Hurricanes and storm recovery

Storm debris, FEMA reimbursement, repairs after hurricanes and storm preparations.

81 governments we follow put 639 items about this on their agendas since January 2025; a vote passed on 111.

Found by fixed rules from each item's official wording (how). Newest first, by government. "On the agenda" means no vote is recorded online, which for many governments we follow is always the case. Follow this subject (RSS feed).

City of Miramar (3)

  1. November 18, 2026

    Change to the city's development rules

    SECOND READING of Temp

    What does “Change to the development rules” mean?

    The Land Development Code is the city's rulebook for building: lot sizes, setbacks, parking, signs and so on. This changes the rulebook itself, for everyone it applies to. More

    Official wording

    SECOND READING of Temp. Ord. #O1865 amending the Land Development Code of the City of Miramar, pursuant to Section 302 of said Land Development Code and Section 166.041, Florida Statutes; more specifically, at Chapter 4, entitled “Zoning,” at Section 405, entitled “Specific Use Regulations,” by repealing and reenacting Subsection 405.21, entitled “Portable Storage Units,” to update the standards for portable storage units on single-family residential lots by extending the allowable duration thereof; streamlining the permit process; allowing for administrative extensions; making findings; providing for purpose, location and number, size and duration, hurricane removal, permits, standards, and enforcement, providing for repeal; providing for severability; providing for interpretation; providing for correction of scrivener’s errors; providing for codification; and providing for an effective date. (Passed 1st Reading on 11/4/26) (Building, Planning & Zoning Senior Planner Robert Artuso)

    On the agenda; no vote recorded.

    Official record: the city's page for this meeting. Find File # #O1865 (agenda item 20.) in its list.

  7. August 5, 2025

    Property tax rates (millage)

    As we enter the peak of hurricane season, residents are reminded that now is the time to trim trees and vegetation in their yards

    What does “Property tax rate (millage)” mean?

    Florida property tax rates are set in “mills.” One mill is $1 of tax for every $1,000 of a property's taxable value. A home with $200,000 of taxable value pays $200 per mill. Source. More

    Official wording

    As we enter the peak of hurricane season, residents are reminded that now is the time to trim trees and vegetation in their yards. Please do not wait until a storm is threatening our area. Residents who may need emergency sheltering during a storm are encouraged now to pre-register for our pet-friendly and medical special needs shelters. Those forms and other hurricane preparedness tips can be found at www.readystlucie.org.
